\subsection{RH readout on the same prime-quadruplet transport}

The short-quadruplet theorem and the moving-coefficient RH criterion
apply to the same probability law. The bridge is a quantitative
bound on its actual local odds. It also clarifies which existential
statement includes an additional prime-pattern conjecture.

\begin{lemma}[Bounded prime reindexing preserves the RH law class]
Let $f$ send every prime $p$ to a prime $q=f(p)$, and suppose
$|q-p|\le H$ for every prime, where $H\ge0$ is fixed. Then
\[
 \left|\frac1{q-1}-\frac1{p-1}\right|
 \le \frac{H(H+1)}{(p-1)^2}.
\]
Thus the weights $w(p)=1/(f(p)-1)$ define a qualifying quadratic
prime law with $\nu=1$ and error constant $H(H+1)$.
Bijectivity is unnecessary for this qualification.
For each fixed such law and every fixed $\alpha>0$, its existing
moving-coefficient reference-sign criterion is equivalent to RH.
All primes, including $2$, belong to the stated domain.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Put $x=p-1\ge1$ and $y=q-1\ge1$. The displacement hypothesis gives
$|x-y|\le H$ and
\[
 x\le y+H\le(H+1)y.
\]
Consequently, keeping the exact reciprocal difference,
\[
 \begin{split}
 \left|\frac1y-\frac1x\right|x^2
   &=|x-y|\frac{x}{y}\\
   &\le H(H+1).
 \end{split}
\]
Division by the positive $x^2$ proves the estimate. The weights
are positive, $\nu=1>0$, and $H(H+1)\ge0$, verifying every field
of the maintained quadratic-law definition. The established
quadratic-law RH equivalence now applies to this fixed law.
No RH hypothesis is used to establish its qualification.
\end{proof}

To state the specialization precisely, let $B\subset\mathbb N$
satisfy
\[
 n\in B\ \Longrightarrow\ n+2\notin B,\qquad
 n\in B\ \Longrightarrow\ p_{n+2}=p_n+6.
\]
Let $e_B$ be the existing permutation exchanging $n$ with $n+2$
for each $n\in B$, fixing all other coordinates. Selected starts
may be adjacent; the first condition prevents conflicting edges.
The associated same-profile probability law is
\[
 a_i=\frac1{p_{e_B(i)}}.
\]
For a prime-labelled coordinate $p=p_i$ its actual local odds are
\[
 w_B(p_i)=\frac{a_i}{1-a_i}=\frac1{p_{e_B(i)}-1}.
\]
Write $\mathcal L_B$ for the quadratic law with these weights.
Its finite coefficients and ratios are
\[
 \begin{split}
 E_{B,N,r}&=[z^r]\prod_{p_i\le N}(1+w_B(p_i)z),\\
 R_{B,N,r}&=\frac{E_{B,N,r-1}}{E_{B,N,r}},\\
 L(N)&=\gamma+\log\log\vartheta(N),\qquad
 r_N=\lfloor\alpha L(N)\rfloor .
 \end{split}
\]
The reference ratio $\mathcal R_{B,r}(u)$ is the maintained
factorial-convolution ratio formed from the entire reference
profile of $\mathcal L_B$. The subscript records its defining law;
the following invariance theorem identifies it with one canonical
reference. The natural number $N$ is an inclusive prime-value
cutoff; $i$ remains the prime index. On the finite observed
probability prefix the earlier notation is $n+1$, with endpoint
$p_n$. No scalar is substituted into the permutation map.

\begin{theorem}[RH on a fixed constellation witness]
For every fixed $B$ satisfying the two conditions above,
$\mathcal L_B$ has $\nu=1$ and error constant $42$.
For every fixed $\alpha>0$,
\[
 \mathrm{RH}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 R_{B,N,r_N}<\mathcal R_{B,r_N}(L(N))
       \quad\hbox{for every sufficiently large }N.
\]
The same fixed $B$ is used throughout the limit.
The coefficient denominators and moving ranks are positive
for all sufficiently large $N$, as provided by the existing
quadratic-law comparison.
Recurrence of the selected blocks is unnecessary for this
fixed-witness equivalence.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
A selected start $i\in B$ is sent to $i+2$, so its prime value
increases by exactly $6$. If $i$ is the upper end of a selected
edge, then $i\ge2$, $i-2\in B$, and its prime value decreases by
exactly $6$. Every other coordinate is fixed. Thus
\[
 |p_{e_B(i)}-p_i|\le6\qquad(i\ge0).
\]
This covers every coordinate, including the indices below two
in the backward-edge test. Apply the preceding lemma with $H=6$:
\[
 |w_B(p)-1/(p-1)|\le42/(p-1)^2.
\]
The odds identity is exact since all moved values are primes
at least $2$. The existing quadratic-law RH theorem, with
$\nu=1$, now gives precisely the displayed moving-rank
equivalence for $\mathcal L_B$.
\end{proof}

Here is how the established sign comparison preserves the zero
information. For this fixed $B,\alpha$, there is $C\ge0$ such
that eventually, with the actual Nicolas log oscillation $F(N)$,
\[
 \begin{array}{rcl}
 F(N)>C/N&\Longrightarrow&
       \mathcal R_{B,r_N}(L(N))<R_{B,N,r_N},\\
 F(N)<-C/N&\Longrightarrow&
       R_{B,N,r_N}<\mathcal R_{B,r_N}(L(N)).
 \end{array}
\]
Under RH the installed Nicolas estimate eventually gives the
second inequality for every fixed $C$. If RH fails, its installed
unbounded positive excursions give $N F(N)>C+1$ arbitrarily far
out, forcing the opposite coefficient sign. This contradicts an
eventual upper-reference sign. The proof uses an unconditional
comparison and the appropriate RH or non-RH Nicolas input in
each direction. The cutoff and the whole signed oscillation are
retained. No uniform cutoff over $B$ or $\alpha$ is asserted.

\begin{corollary}[The exact existential constellation statement]
Fix $\alpha>0$. The following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
\item RH holds and short prime quadruplets recur arbitrarily far out.
\item There is a set $B$ satisfying the two support conditions above,
with arbitrarily large $k$ for which $k,k+1\in B$, and whose
actual law $\mathcal L_B$ has
$R_{B,N,r_N}<\mathcal R_{B,r_N}(L(N))$ eventually.
\end{enumerate}
The short quadruplets are the two already defined consecutive-prime
families
\[
 (p,p+2,p+6,p+8),\qquad (p,p+4,p+6,p+10),\qquad p>3.
\]
No disjointness assumption is imposed on their arithmetic
occurrences. The support construction selects separated
four-coordinate blocks when constructing its one permutation.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
If RH and recurrence hold, the maintained support-selection theorem
supplies $B$ with unbounded adjacent selected starts. The fixed-law
RH equivalence gives its eventual coefficient sign.

Conversely, the coefficient sign gives RH by that same equivalence.
An adjacent selected pair $k,k+1$ forces two coordinates across the
prefix cutoff $k+2$. These double crossings recur arbitrarily far
out. The maintained signed transport estimate gives, for every
$\varepsilon>0$, eventually and uniformly over all positive probes,
\[
 D_{k+1}(t;e_B)\le12+\varepsilon.
\]
Take $\varepsilon=1$ and the constant probe $t=1$. The resulting
eventual ceiling $13<14$, together with recurrent double crossings,
satisfies every hypothesis of the existing quadruplet inverse
theorem. It yields recurrent short quadruplets. In particular the
critical cost and the coefficient-sign readout concern the same
transport, rather than independently chosen probability laws.
\end{proof}

This establishes an RH connection for the constellation witness.
It does not identify RH with quadruplet recurrence alone: the
existential criterion above detects their conjunction. The cost
threshold $12$ records local four-prime geometry; the moving
coefficient sign records the Nicolas oscillation. The full
Bernoulli profile alone does not distinguish these transports,
since it is invariant under coordinate permutations.

\begin{flushleft}
The reciprocal displacement bound, complete quadratic-law adapter,
prime-index conversion and bounded-reindexing RH criterion are
compiled in
\path{Helpers/PrimeProfileBoundedReindexing.lean}.
The complete distance-six verification, actual probability-odds
identity, explicit law constants, fixed-witness RH equivalence and
existential conjunction criterion are compiled in
\path{Assembly/PrimeProfileQuadrupletRH.lean}, exported through
\path{Assembly/ProbabilityProfiles.lean}.
The existing short-quadruplet attainment, support selection and
inverse theorem are consumed without a new prime-pattern supply
assumption. No external-priority claim is made. The certified
zero-free boundary remains $7/8$.
\end{flushleft}


\clearpage
\subsection{One canonical RH reference for every constellation transport}

The entire reference in the preceding criterion is independent of the
chosen block support. This follows from an exact infinite signed pairing,
using the quadratic bounds to justify the complete series.

\begin{theorem}[Involutive invariance of the entire reference]
Let $\mathcal A,\mathcal B$ be qualifying quadratic prime laws with
the same parameter $\nu$. Suppose $\sigma$ is an involutive permutation
of the primes and
\[
 w_{\mathcal A}(p)=w_{\mathcal B}(\sigma(p))
       \qquad\hbox{for every prime }p.
\]
Then their entire reference profiles are equal. Consequently all
reference Taylor coefficients are equal, including degree zero.
Their factorial-convolution reference ratios agree wherever the common
denominator is nonzero.
The two laws may have different quadratic error constants.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For a fixed real $t\ge0$, define the complete signed difference
\[
 \begin{split}
 g_t(p)
 &=\left[\log(1+w_{\mathcal A}(p)t)
           -\nu t\log(1+1/(p-1))\right]\\
 &\quad-\left[\log(1+w_{\mathcal B}(p)t)
           -\nu t\log(1+1/(p-1))\right]\\
 &=\log(1+w_{\mathcal B}(\sigma(p))t)
                  -\log(1+w_{\mathcal B}(p)t).
 \end{split}
\]
Each bracketed profile series is absolutely convergent by its proved
quadratic-law logarithmic bound. Their difference is therefore summable.
The normalizing terms have been canceled exactly. Involutivity gives
\[
 g_t(\sigma(p))=-g_t(p).
\]
Reindex only this summable difference:
\[
 \sum_p g_t(p)=\sum_p g_t(\sigma(p))
             =-\sum_p g_t(p).
\]
Its sum is zero. The two logarithmic reference profiles are thus equal
for every $t\ge0$. Exponentiating and applying the existing positive-real
product identity shows equality of their entire profiles on the
nonnegative real axis.

Both reference profiles are entire by the established quadratic-law
construction. Their coincidence set has $0$ as an accumulation point:
for every $\varepsilon>0$, the nonzero real point $\varepsilon/2$
lies within distance $\varepsilon$ of zero and has equal profile values.
The analytic identity principle therefore gives equality on the whole
complex plane. Every Taylor coefficient at zero is equal. Substitution
in the finite factorial convolutions proves the reference-ratio equality
on its stated nonzero-denominator domain.
\end{proof}

Only the signed difference is reindexed in this proof. In particular,
the argument does not rearrange either divergent positive sum
$\sum_p\log(1+w_{\mathcal A}(p)t)$ or
$\sum_p\log(1+w_{\mathcal B}(p)t)$ separately.

Let $\mathcal L_0$ be the fixed canonical law with
\[
 w_0(p)=\frac1{p-1},\qquad \nu=1,\qquad\hbox{quadratic error }0,
\]
and denote its reference ratio by $\mathcal R_{0,r}(u)$.

\begin{corollary}[A common canonical constellation RH criterion]
For every fixed support $B$ from the preceding subsection,
\[
 \Phi_{\mathcal L_B}=\Phi_{\mathcal L_0},\qquad
 \mathcal R_{B,r}(u)=\mathcal R_{0,r}(u)
\]
on the common nonzero-denominator domain of the ratios. The reference
clock is exactly the same at every cutoff. For each fixed $\alpha>0$,
\[
 \mathrm{RH}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 R_{B,N,r_N}<\mathcal R_{0,r_N}(L(N))
       \quad\hbox{eventually in }N.
\]
Thus the reference profile, its coefficients, its clock and its ratio
are fixed independently of $B$. The actual finite coefficient ratio
$R_{B,N,r_N}$ still belongs to the chosen constellation transport.
The existential criterion of the preceding subsection remains valid
with $\mathcal R_0$ replacing every $\mathcal R_B$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The exact correspondence $i\mapsto p_i$ identifies the permutation
$e_B$ with an involutive permutation of the primes. Its inverse
coordinate is the number of primes strictly below the prime label;
the two inverse identities hold on the entire enumeration, beginning
at $p_0=2$. The actual odds satisfy
\[
 w_B(p_i)=w_0(p_{e_B(i)}).
\]
Both laws qualify: $\mathcal L_B$ has the already proved error bound
$42$, and $\mathcal L_0$ has error zero; both have $\nu=1$.
Apply the theorem to obtain equality of the entire profiles and every
reference coefficient and ratio. The clock identity follows directly
from their common $\nu=1$. Substitution into the already compiled
fixed-witness and existential RH equivalences gives the assertions.
Their eventual positive-denominator domain is retained.
\end{proof}

The infinite signed cancellation here removes the change in the
reference caused by reindexing. The finite-cutoff coefficient ratio,
the complete compensated cutoff cost, and the Nicolas clock oscillation
remain in their respective statements. In particular the RH sign
response is preserved; no zero signal is set to zero by this pairing.
The cost threshold continues to distinguish the local prime geometry
even though these entire references coincide.

\begin{flushleft}
The summable logarithmic pairing, entire-function equality, equality of
all reference coefficients and ratios, and exact prime-coordinate
conversion are compiled in
\path{Helpers/PrimeProfileReferenceInvariance.lean}.
Their application to the actual quadruplet law, the canonical clock,
the fixed-reference RH criterion and the fixed-reference existential
criterion are compiled in
\path{Assembly/PrimeProfileQuadrupletCanonicalRH.lean},
exported through \path{Assembly/ProbabilityProfiles.lean}.
The preceding corollary concerns each fixed support and rank parameter.
The next theorem makes the cutoff uniform over all supports, and more
generally over every prime permutation of a fixed displacement radius.
No uniformity in a growing rank parameter is asserted.
\end{flushleft}

\subsection{A uniform RH criterion over bounded prime permutations}

Write $P_N=\{p\text{ prime}:p\le N\}$ and $w_0(p)=1/(p-1)$.
For a permutation $\sigma$ of the primes put
\[
 S_{\sigma,N}=\sigma(P_N),\qquad
 E_{\sigma,N,r}=e_r\bigl((w_0(p))_{p\in S_{\sigma,N}}\bigr),\qquad
 R_{\sigma,N,r}=\frac{E_{\sigma,N,r-1}}{E_{\sigma,N,r}}.
\]
Thus these are exactly the coefficients obtained by assigning odds
$w_0(\sigma(p))$ to the sampled prime coordinate $p\le N$.
For the identity permutation write $E_{0,N,r}$ and $R_{0,N,r}$.
The benchmark remains the fixed canonical reference
$\mathcal R_{0,r}(u)$, rather than a reference chosen after $\sigma$.
Set
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 A(N)=\sum_{p\in P_N}\log(1+w_0(p)),\\
 L(N)=\gamma+\log\log\vartheta(N),\qquad
 r_N=\lfloor\alpha L(N)\rfloor .
 \end{gathered}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Uniform bounded-displacement RH criterion]
Fix $H\in\mathbb N$ and $\alpha>0$. Let $\mathcal S_H$ be the
class of all permutations of the primes satisfying
$|\sigma(p)-p|\le H$ at every prime. Then
\[
 \mathrm{RH}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \exists N_0\ \forall N\ge N_0\ \forall\sigma\in\mathcal S_H:
 \quad R_{\sigma,N,r_N}<\mathcal R_{0,r_N}(L(N)).
\]
Under RH one may choose this same $N_0$ so that $r_N>0$,
all the displayed actual denominators, and the canonical reference
denominator are positive. The cutoff depends on $H$ and $\alpha$,
and is independent of $\sigma$. The theorem includes $H=0$ and
permutations with cycles of arbitrary length; no involution or prime-gap
recurrence premise is required.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
We first establish two exact finite comparisons.

If $H\le N$, bounded displacement applied to $\sigma$ and its inverse
gives
\[
 P_{N-H}\subseteq S_{\sigma,N}\subseteq P_{N+H}.
\]
Indeed, $p\le N$ implies $\sigma(p)\le N+H$.
If $q\le N-H$ and $p=\sigma^{-1}(q)$, then $p\le q+H\le N$,
so $q\in S_{\sigma,N}$. This accounts for every crossing coordinate,
including those at the endpoints.

Adding a nonnegative coordinate to a finite nonnegative vector can
only decrease $e_{r-1}/e_r$ wherever its initial denominator is positive.
To see the sign, write $a\ge0$ for the new coordinate and use
$e_j^{\rm new}=e_j+a e_{j-1}$, with $e_{-1}=0$.
After multiplying the positive denominators, the assertion reduces to
\[
 a(e_{r-1}^2-e_{r-2}e_r)\ge0.
\]
The established elementary-symmetric log-concavity inequality gives
this for $r\ge2$; for $r=1$ the numerator is $e_0=1$ and the
denominator increases. Iteration over the finite complement proves
\[
 R_{0,N+H,r}\le R_{\sigma,N,r}\le R_{0,N-H,r}
 \tag{U1}
\]
whenever $r\ge1$ and $E_{0,N-H,r}>0$.
All intermediate and larger-prefix denominators remain positive.
The maintained proof first works over the nonnegative rational odds
and then transports the exact symmetric coefficients and inequalities
to $\mathbb R$.

Next, for every $M\ge1$ the complete clock correction satisfies
\[
 \begin{split}
 0\le A(M+H)-A(M)
 &=\sum_{\substack{p\text{ prime}\\M<p\le M+H}}
       \log\left(1+\frac1{p-1}\right)\\
 &\le \frac{H}{M}.
 \end{split}
 \tag{U2}
\]
There are at most $H$ integers in the interval, and each summand is
nonnegative and at most $1/(p-1)\le1/M$.
No prime, logarithmic term, or positive correction is discarded.
The argument includes $H=0$ and the prime $2$.
In particular, for fixed $H$ this increment tends to zero.

Apply the existing moving-clock coefficient-sign theorem to the single
canonical law, at prefix $M$, with arithmetic clock $A(M)$ and reference
clock $L(M+H)$. Its hypotheses hold: $L(M+H)\to\infty$, and
\[
 A(M)-L(M+H)
 =[A(M+H)-L(M+H)]-[A(M+H)-A(M)]\longrightarrow0.
\]
It supplies a constant $C\ge0$, depending only on $H,\alpha$ and the
canonical law, and an eventual domain with positive rank and
denominators, on which
\[
 L(M+H)-A(M)<-\frac C M
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 R_{0,M,r_{M+H}}<
   \mathcal R_{0,r_{M+H}}(L(M+H)).
 \tag{U3}
\]
Its finite factorial convolutions are the actual elementary symmetric
coefficients by the proved generating-product identity.

Assume RH. Write $F(N)=L(N)-A(N)$ on the eventual domain
$\vartheta(N)>1$. The established Nicolas consequence of RH gives
$F(N)<-K/N$ eventually for every fixed real $K$.
Choose $K=2(C+H+1)$ and take $M\ge\max(1,H)$, $N=M+H$.
Then $N\le2M$, and (U2) gives the fully signed estimate
\[
 \begin{split}
 L(N)-A(M)
 &= F(N)+[A(N)-A(M)]\\
 &<-\frac{2(C+H+1)}{N}+\frac H M\\
 &\le-\frac{C+H+1}{M}+\frac H M
   =-\frac C M-\frac1M<-\frac C M.
 \end{split}
\]
Thus (U3) applies. Combining it with the upper inequality in (U1)
proves the desired strict inequality for every $\sigma\in\mathcal S_H$.
Every threshold used here concerns only $M,H,\alpha$ and the canonical
law. Replacing $M$ by $N-H$ therefore yields one eventual cutoff for
the entire class, with all positivity conclusions retained.

Conversely, the identity permutation belongs to $\mathcal S_H$ for
every $H\ge0$. Selecting it in the asserted eventual inequality gives
the canonical moving-rank coefficient criterion, whose already proved
converse implies RH. This also proves nonvacuity at $H=0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[One cutoff for all constellation supports]
For each fixed $\alpha>0$, RH is equivalent to the eventual inequality
\[
 R_{B,N,r_N}<\mathcal R_{0,r_N}(L(N))
\]
holding simultaneously for every support $B$ satisfying the separation
and distance-six hypotheses of the quadruplet construction.
Under RH the actual denominators are positive on this common domain.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The proved all-coordinate displacement theorem bounds the associated
prime permutation by $6$, in both swap directions and at the small
indices. Apply the uniform theorem with $H=6$; its actual finite odds
are exactly those of $\mathcal L_B$.
For the converse select the empty support, which satisfies both support
hypotheses and gives the canonical criterion. Equivalently, apply the
already proved fixed-support RH converse to that support.
\end{proof}

The quantifiers are stronger than
$\forall B\,\exists N_0(B)\,\forall N\ge N_0(B)$:
here $\exists N_0\,\forall N\ge N_0\,\forall B$.
Consequently the same sign test also permits an admissible support or
permutation chosen separately at each cutoff.
The theorem does not assert uniformity for $H=H(N)$ or
$\alpha=\alpha(N)$. It supplies no constellation occurrence by itself:
adding a recurrent-witness requirement retains the earlier conjunction
of RH with constellation recurrence. The canonical zero signal is
retained through the signed clock correction. No zero-free exponent
gain is claimed; the certified boundary remains $7/8$.

The finite rational and real ratio comparisons and the exact
prime-prefix sandwich are compiled in
\path{Assembly/PrimeProfilePrefixSandwich.lean}.
The $H/M$ clock bound and shifted-clock limits are compiled in
\path{Helpers/PrimeProfileClockShift.lean}.
The uniform RH equivalence, its positive-denominator forward theorem
and the simultaneous quadruplet specialization are compiled in
\path{Assembly/PrimeProfileUniformConstellationRH.lean},
exported through \path{Assembly/ProbabilityProfiles.lean}.
\end{flushleft}

\subsection{Shared excursions and every prescribed constellation shape}

The uniform criterion has a stronger converse than selecting the identity.
A false-RH excursion is visible simultaneously through every bounded
prime permutation. This will supply an RH converse for a single
shape-specific transport, including cycles longer than two.

\begin{theorem}[Shared positive excursions]
Fix $H\in\mathbb N$ and $\alpha>0$. If RH is false, then
arbitrarily large integer cutoffs $N$ satisfy
\[
 \mathcal R_{0,r_N}(L(N))<R_{\sigma,N,r_N}
       \qquad\text{for every }\sigma\in\mathcal S_H
\]
simultaneously. At these cutoffs $r_N>0$ and all actual and reference
denominators are positive.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The past reference clock $L(M-H)$ tends to infinity. Moreover
\[
 A(M)-L(M-H)
 =[A(M-H)-L(M-H)]+[A(M)-A(M-H)]\longrightarrow0.
\]
For the second term apply (U2) at $M-H$, using
$(M-H)+H=M$ eventually. Both assertions include $H=0$.

Apply the canonical moving-clock sign theorem at prefix $M$ with
reference clock $L(M-H)$. It supplies $C\ge0$ and an eventual domain
on which a positive clock difference greater than $C/M$ forces the
actual ratio above the reference. Put $M=N+H$.
The fully retained signed difference is
\[
 L(N)-A(N+H)=F(N)-[A(N+H)-A(N)].
\]
Set $K=C+H+1$. If $N\ge1$ and $F(N)>K/N$, then
\[
 \begin{split}
 L(N)-A(N+H)
 &>\frac{C+H+1}{N}-\frac HN\\
 &=\frac CN+\frac1N
   >\frac C{N+H}.
 \end{split}
\]
Thus the canonical ratio at $N+H$, with rank $r_N$, exceeds the
reference. The lower inequality in (U1) transfers the same strict
inequality to every $\sigma\in\mathcal S_H$.

For completeness, the denominator required by (U1) is available
unconditionally: the canonical moving-clock theorem with prefix $N-H$
and reference clock $L(N)$ supplies positivity of
$E_{0,N-H,r_N}$ and the reference denominator eventually, by the
forward-shift clock limits already proved. Their finite superset
comparisons supply every actual denominator.

If RH is false, the proved Nicolas excursion theorem gives, beyond
every threshold, an integer $N$ with $NF(N)>K$.
Choose $N$ beyond all the preceding eventual domains.
Dividing by the positive $N$ gives the required hypothesis.
Neither $C$, $K$, nor the selected cutoff depends on $\sigma$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Every individual bounded permutation reads RH]
For any fixed $\sigma\in\mathcal S_H$ and $\alpha>0$,
\[
 \mathrm{RH}
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 R_{\sigma,N,r_N}<\mathcal R_{0,r_N}(L(N))
                      \quad\text{eventually in }N.
\]
Even the nonstrict eventual upper inequality implies RH.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The uniform RH theorem gives the forward implication.
If RH were false, the shared excursions would violate the asserted
eventual upper inequality for this same $\sigma$.
The argument uses its actual coefficients against the canonical
reference, without assuming that $\sigma$ is an involution.
\end{proof}

We now retain a complete finite constellation shape.
Let $k\ge2$, let $h_0,\ldots,h_{k-1}$ be distinct nonnegative integers,
and suppose $h_j\le W$ for every $j$.
The usual normalized description is
$0=h_0<h_1<\cdots<h_{k-1}=W$; the formal theorem only needs
injectivity of the offset list and its bound by $W$.
Write $\operatorname{Rec}(\mathcal H)$ for
\[
 \forall X\in\mathbb N\ \exists q\ge X:
          \quad q+h_j\text{ is prime for every }j<k.
\]
A \emph{full-shape cycle at $q$} in a prime permutation $\sigma$
means that there are prime labels $a_0,\ldots,a_{k-1}$ satisfying
\[
 a_j=q+h_j,\qquad
 \sigma(a_j)=a_{j+1\bmod k}\qquad(0\le j<k).
\]
Distinctness of the offsets gives distinct labels.
This definition records the whole shape, including its cyclic order.

\begin{theorem}[Arbitrary-shape constellation and RH equivalence]
Fix the finite shape above and $\alpha>0$. Then the following are
equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
\item RH holds and $\operatorname{Rec}(\mathcal H)$ holds.
\item There is a permutation $\sigma$ of the primes with
$|\sigma(p)-p|\le W$ for every prime, with full-shape cycles at
arbitrarily large anchors $q$, such that
\[
 R_{\sigma,N,r_N}<\mathcal R_{0,r_N}(L(N))
                      \quad\text{eventually in }N.
\]
\end{enumerate}
The construction in the forward direction can use disjoint intervals
$[q,q+W]$ and fix every prime outside the selected copies.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Assume recurrence of the shape. Apply the proved separated-subset
selection theorem to its set of anchors, with separation $W+1$.
This yields an unbounded set $A$ of valid anchors such that
\[
 a<b,\ a,b\in A\quad\Longrightarrow\quad a+W<b.
\]
For $a\in A$ and $j<k$, define the prime label
$\iota(a,j)=a+h_j$.
This map from $A\times\{0,\ldots,k-1\}$ to the primes is injective.
Indeed, if $a<b$, then
$a+h_i\le a+W<b\le b+h_j$, excluding equality.
The reverse ordering is excluded in the same way.
Equal anchors then force equal offsets, and injectivity of the
offset list forces equal indices.

Rotate the second coordinate of this labelled set and extend the
resulting permutation along $\iota$, fixing its complement.
The established embedding-extension API gives a genuine permutation
of all primes, with
\[
 \sigma(\iota(a,j))=\iota(a,j+1\bmod k).
\]
On a selected copy the anchor cancels from the displacement:
\[
 |\sigma(\iota(a,j))-\iota(a,j)|
      =|h_{j+1\bmod k}-h_j|\le W.
\]
On the complement the displacement is zero.
Every selected anchor gives its full-shape cycle, and the anchors
are unbounded. Under RH, the preceding individual-permutation
criterion supplies its eventual canonical coefficient sign.

Conversely, a full-shape cycle supplies prime labels equal to every
$q+h_j$. Arbitrarily large cycle anchors therefore imply
$\operatorname{Rec}(\mathcal H)$, with every offset retained.
The eventual coefficient sign for this one bounded permutation implies
RH by the preceding corollary. This proves both required conclusions.
\end{proof}

The construction starts with all occurrences and selects disjoint copies;
it never assumes that the original occurrences do not overlap.
No consecutive-prime condition is imposed: intervening primes may exist.
If consecutive patterns are desired, their absence-of-extra-primes
condition is additional geometric information. The analytic sign cutoff,
when RH holds, depends only on $W$ and $\alpha$, and works for every
permitted shape and cycle transport inside that displacement class.
No uniformity for a growing $W$ or a growing $\alpha$ is claimed.

For a compact comparison, let $s_\sigma(N)$ denote the canonical
coefficient inequality at cutoff $N$, let $E_\sigma$ denote its eventual
validity, and let $C_{\mathcal H}(\sigma)$ denote recurrence of full-shape
cycles. For every fixed $\sigma\in\mathcal S_H$ we have
\[
 \mathrm{RH}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ E_{\rm id}
              \ \Longleftrightarrow\ E_\sigma .
\]
The uniform and shape-specific statements are
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathrm{RH}
 &\ \Longleftrightarrow\
       \exists N_0\ \forall N\ge N_0\ \forall\sigma\in\mathcal S_H:
          s_\sigma(N),\\
 \mathrm{RH}\land\operatorname{Rec}(\mathcal H)
 &\ \Longleftrightarrow\
       \exists\sigma\in\mathcal S_W:
           C_{\mathcal H}(\sigma)\land E_\sigma .
 \end{aligned}
\]
The smallest-recurring-gap location theorem records one coarse
geometric parameter. The full-shape cycle word records an arbitrary
finite geometric pattern. The centered sign tests the common global
Nicolas bias, which cannot be hidden by choosing a different bounded
transport: false RH produces shared positive excursions.
These are separate requirements in the equivalence; no logical
independence theorem is asserted. An independently proved eventual
sign for one such witness would imply RH. Shape recurrence alone
does not supply that sign, and RH alone does not supply shape recurrence.
The certified zero-free boundary remains $7/8$.

\begin{flushleft}
The past-clock limits are compiled in

\subsection{Prime progressions with unbounded additive diameter}

The fixed-shape theorem requires recurrence of one prescribed offset vector.
Prime arithmetic progressions offer a different, established supply: the
length is prescribed, while the common difference may vary. We retain this
distinction throughout.

Consider finite blocks of primes
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 B_i=\{p_{i,0}<\cdots<p_{i,k_i-1}\},\qquad k_i\ge2,\\
 p_{i,k_i-1}<p_{i+1,0},\qquad p_{i,k_i-1}\le2p_{i,0}.
 \end{gathered}
\]
Rotate each block in increasing order, sending its last element to its first,
and fix every other prime. This defines a permutation $\sigma$; the block
lengths and additive displacements may be unbounded. Put
\[
 P_{\sigma,N}(z)=\prod_{p\le N}\left(1+\frac{z}{\sigma(p)-1}\right),
 \qquad Q_{\sigma,N}(z)=P_{\sigma,N}(z)e^{-A(N)z},
\]
where $A(N)$ is the canonical arithmetic clock. Write $P_{0,N}$ and $Q_{0,N}$
for the identity permutation, and $\Phi_0$ for the canonical entire profile.

\begin{theorem}[One-boundary stability]\label{thm:ordered-cycle-boundary}
For every fixed $R\ge0$, the normalized products satisfy
\[
 \sup_{|z|\le R}|Q_{\sigma,N}(z)-\Phi_0(z)|
 \le \frac{C_R}{N}
\]
for all sufficiently large $N$. No upper bound on $k_i$ or on the additive
diameter is needed. Consequently, for each fixed $b\ge0$ there are
$r_0,N_0,K$ such that, for $r\ge r_0$, $N\ge N_0$, $u>0$ and $r/u\le b$,
the finite and reference factorial-convolution denominators are positive
and their adjacent coefficient ratios differ by at most $K/(rN)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
All complete blocks cancel exactly. If a block is incomplete at $N$, let
$p_0$ be its first prime and $p_m$ its first prime exceeding $N$.
The included primes form an initial segment, whose shifted product obeys
\[
 P_{\sigma,N}(z)(1+z/(p_0-1))
   =P_{0,N}(z)(1+z/(p_m-1)).
\]
This identity is valid at zeros of either full product. Define instead the
boundary multiplier
\[
 D_N(z)=\frac{1+z/(p_m-1)}{1+z/(p_0-1)}.
\]
If no block is incomplete, set $D_N=1$. In the incomplete case,
$p_0\le N<p_m\le2p_0$, so
\[
 0\le\frac1{p_m-1}\le\frac1{p_0-1}\le\frac4N.
\]
For $N\ge\max(1,8R)$ and $|z|\le R$, the boundary denominator has norm
at least $1/2$. Thus
\[
 |D_N(z)-1|
 =\left|\frac{(1/(p_m-1)-1/(p_0-1))z}{1+z/(p_0-1)}\right|
 \le\frac{8R}{N}.
\]
The cross-multiplied identity now gives
$Q_{\sigma,N}=Q_{0,N}D_N$ without dividing by $P_{0,N}$.
If $|Q_{0,N}|\le B_R$ and
$|Q_{0,N}-\Phi_0|\le C_{0,R}/N$, then
\[
 |Q_{\sigma,N}-\Phi_0|
 \le\frac{C_{0,R}+8RB_R}{N}.
\]
The canonical prefix bound and profile approximation supply these constants.

Cauchy's estimate on a disc of radius $b+1$ bounds every Taylor coefficient
error by $C/[N(b+1)^j]$. The geometric descending-factorial estimates give
$O(1/N)$ errors for both the normalized coefficient polynomial and its first
derivative. The positive canonical lower bound controls their denominators.
In the exact adjacent-ratio identity the common leading term cancels,
leaving $(r/u)^2/r$ times a difference of logarithmic derivatives.
This retains the bound $K/(rN)$, rather than $O(1/N)$.
The initial cutoff is allowed to depend on the chosen radius throughout.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Progression transports retain the RH criterion]
\label{cor:prime-progression-transport-rh}
Fix $\alpha>0$ and the above permutation $\sigma$. Let
$r_N=\lfloor\alpha L(N)\rfloor$, let $R_\sigma(N,r)$ denote the adjacent
coefficient ratio of $P_{\sigma,N}$, and use the canonical reference ratio
$\overline R_0$. Then
\[
 \mathrm{RH}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 R_\sigma(N,r_N)<\overline R_0(r_N,L(N))
 \quad\hbox{for every sufficiently large }N.
\]
The ranks and both denominators are positive eventually.
There are such permutations whose blocks are prime arithmetic progressions
of lengths tending to infinity.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The preceding inverse-rank comparison and the canonical reference secant
preserve both clock signs whenever $|L(N)-A(N)|>C/N$.
Under RH the proved Nicolas estimate gives
$L(N)-A(N)<-C/N$ eventually, for every fixed $C$.
If RH fails, $N(L(N)-A(N))$ has arbitrarily late positive excursions
exceeding every fixed constant. These force the reverse strict coefficient
inequality and contradict even an eventual non-strict upper sign.
The factorial-convolution coefficients at $A(N)$ are the coefficients of
$P_{\sigma,N}$, since $P_{\sigma,N}=Q_{\sigma,N}e^{A(N)z}$.

For the progression supply, use Green--Tao's theorem on arbitrarily long
prime progressions beyond any prescribed threshold.\footnote{B.~Green and
T.~Tao, \emph{The primes contain arbitrarily long arithmetic progressions},
Annals of Mathematics 167 (2008), 481--547, Theorems~1.1 and~1.2,
\url{https://doi.org/10.4007/annals.2008.167.481}.}
From a progression of length $2k$, retain its latter $k$ terms.
If its first retained prime is $p_0=a+kd$, its last is
$a+(2k-1)d<2p_0$. Choose these blocks successively beyond the preceding
last prime and with $k$ tending to infinity. Their disjoint cycles give
the required permutation. This does not produce recurrence at a fixed
common difference.
\end{proof}

The same ordinary construction works inside a fixed reduced residue class:
its prime subset has positive relative density, so Green--Tao's
Theorem~1.2 applies there. Every prime outside the chosen blocks remains
fixed. The observable still uses all primes and the canonical RH reference.
